Soap and selected nonionic detergent — practical record

Science Atlas 360° · VisionariesAI Labs Private Limited · CL-0110

Aim and prediction

Compare sodium oleate and a selected neutral detergent in soft and hard water. Distinguish equal-molar from equal-solute-mass dose matching.

Apparatus and represented chemistry

Four matched sample vessels, separate finite 20 mL stocks, metered delivery and matched agitation. Soap: sodium oleate. Neutral-detergent reference: octyl β-D-glucopyranoside. M²⁺ + 2 oleate⁻ → M(oleate)₂ equivalents is represented only for the soap channel.

Procedure

  1. State the aim: compare the selected sodium-oleate soap and neutral octyl-glucoside reference in soft and prepared hard water.
  2. Select equal-molar or equal-solute-mass stock matching. Record actual stock molarity and g/L for each surfactant; equal stock volumes alone do not establish equal dose.
  3. Record zero-dose observations for all four preparations. Keep sample volume, hard-water ion concentrations and delivered volumes matched.
  4. Increase delivered dose in recorded steps. Pause and record surfactant bound/unprecipitated, remaining hardness and product-equivalent mass for all four cells.
  5. Apply matched agitation for the visual comparison. Do not infer measured foam height, micelle counts or cleaning efficiency from the animation.
  6. Show dose conversion, the soap 2:1 anion-to-divalent-ion ratio, dose-basis conversion, hardness substitution and material/charge accounts with units.
  7. Repeat with the other dose-matching basis. Compare which quantities remain equal and which change.
  8. Write a result using table values. Explain why the neutral-detergent reference can remain unprecipitated while hardness ions remain in solution; qualify the conclusion to this model.

Result and conclusion

In this ideal reference the soap channel consumes oleate and divalent ions. The selected neutral detergent does not remove the hardness ions through that channel. Availability is not a measured cleansing efficiency.

Precautions and interpretation limits

  • This selected comparison represents sodium oleate and neutral octyl β-D-glucopyranoside; it does not represent all commercial soaps or detergent formulations.
  • Soap precipitation is ideal and complete with proportional calcium/magnesium allocation when soap limits. No Ksp preference, pH, kinetic or temperature model is used.
  • No divalent carboxylate channel is assigned to the selected neutral detergent. Other associations, salt effects and clouding are omitted; this is not a universal claim of immunity to hard water.
  • Unprecipitated surfactant is an inventory, not necessarily free monomers. No aggregate count, micelle size, foam height, cleansing efficiency or surface tension is predicted.
  • The 20–25 mmol/L nonionic CMC band is a published reference at 20–25 °C. Position relative to that band does not establish the CMC in this salt-containing mixture.
  • Prepared-solution density is selected as 1 g/mL; nominal prepared volumes add. Carrier mass excludes dissolved solute so it is not double-counted. Precipitate volume is not inferred.
